\item Under the hypotheses of b), let $C$ be a closed subscheme of dimension $1$ of $Z$ such that $Y\cap C=s$, and let $\widetilde C$ be the strict localization of $C$ at $\bar s$. Then the restriction arrow
\begin{equation}
\mathrm R\Gamma(Z-Y,i'_!L)
\longrightarrow
\mathrm R\Gamma(\widetilde C-\bar s,i'_!(L)|\widetilde C-\bar s)
\tag{4.3.2}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:4.3.2}
\end{equation}
is zero.
\end{enumerate}
\end{statement}

The trivial isomorphism
\[
i^*Rj_*i'_!L\xrightarrow{\sim}Rj_*(i'^*i'_!L)=Rj_*L
\]
gives an isomorphism
\[
m'^*Rj_*i'_!L\xrightarrow{\sim} i_1^*m^*Rj_*i'_!L,
\]
hence, by adjunction, an arrow
\[
i_{1!}m'^*Rj_*i'_!L\longrightarrow m^*Rj_*(i'_!L),
\]
which induces an isomorphism outside $z$. We show that it is an isomorphism, that is, that if $\bar z$ is a geometric point above $z$, then
\[
(Rj_*(i'_!L))_{\bar z}=0.
\]
For this, one may replace $\Lambda$ by $\mathbb Z/\ell^\nu$, where $\ell^\nu$ annihilates $\Lambda$, and by dévissage and passage to the limit reduce to the case where $L$ is a locally constant $\mathbb Z/\ell^\nu$-module of finite type corresponding to a representation of $\pi_1$ through an $\ell$-group. Since the only finite abelian group of $\ell$-torsion which is simple under the action of an $\ell$-group is $\mathbb Z/\ell$ with trivial action, a further dévissage reduces to the case where $L$ is the constant sheaf $\mathbb Z/\ell$.

Let $\widetilde Z$ be the strict localization of $Z$ at $\bar z$, $\widetilde X=X\times_Z\widetilde Z$, $\widetilde Y=Y\times_Z\widetilde Z$, and let $i':\widetilde Z-(\widetilde X\cup\widetilde Y)\hookrightarrow \widetilde Z-\widetilde Y$ be the inclusion. One has
\[
(Rj_*(i'_!(\mathbb Z/\ell)))_{\bar z}
=\mathrm R\Gamma(\widetilde Z-\widetilde Y,i'_!(\mathbb Z/\ell)),
\]
so it remains to prove that the restriction arrow
\[
H^n(\widetilde Z-\widetilde Y,\mathbb Z/\ell)
\longrightarrow
H^n(\widetilde X-\bar z,\mathbb Z/\ell)
\]
is an isomorphism for all $n$. For $n\le1$ this is a consequence of Abhyankar's lemma, and for $n>1$ both sides vanish by the purity hypothesis. The first assertion of a) is therefore proved. We prove the second. By dévissage, we may suppose $L$ concentrated in degree $0$. The hypothesis (P) implies, by dévissage, that $R^qj_*L=0$ for $q>2$. It remains to prove constructibility and local constancy of $m'^*R^qj_*i'_!L$ for $q\le1$, which is standard. For constructibility, resolving $L$ on the right by sheaves of the form $p_*p^*L$, where $p:Z'\to Z$ is a finite covering tamely ramified along $Y$, reduces to the case where $L$ is constant, of value $L_0$: then
\[
m'^*j_*i'_!L=(L_0)_{Y-z},
\qquad
m'^*R^1j_*i'_!L=(L_0)_{Y-z}(-1)
\]
by Abhyankar. For local constancy, one has to see that the specialization arrows are isomorphisms. One may, for this, replace $\Lambda$ by $\mathbb Z/\ell^\nu$ as above, and then reduce by dévissage to the case where $L$ is the constant sheaf $\mathbb Z/\ell$; the conclusion follows from Abhyankar's lemma.

We have therefore proved a). We prove b). Let $\bar\eta$ be a geometric generic point of $Y$. Choose specialization arrows
\[
a:\bar\eta\to Y(\bar z),\qquad b:\bar\eta\to Y(\bar s),
\]
where $Y(\bar z)$ (resp. $Y(\bar s)$) is the strict localization of $Y$ at $\bar z$ (resp. $\bar s$). Put
\[
M=m^*Rj_*(i'_!L).
\]
The commutative square
\[
\begin{tikzcd}
Y \arrow[r] & Y(\bar s)\\
Y(\bar z) \arrow[u] \arrow[r,"a"'] & \bar\eta \arrow[u,"b"']
\end{tikzcd}
\]
induces a commutative square
\[
\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=large,row sep=large]
\mathrm R\Gamma(Y,M) \arrow[r,"{\hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:4.3.1]{4.3.1}}"] \arrow[d] & M_{\bar s} \arrow[d,"b^*"]\\
M_{\bar z} \arrow[r,"a^*"'] & M_{\bar\eta}.
\end{tikzcd}
\]
Since $M|Y-z$ has locally constant cohomology, $b^*$ is an isomorphism. On the other hand, since $M=i_{1!}i_1^*M$, one has $M_{\bar z}=0$, hence the arrow \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:4.3.1]{4.3.1} is zero. It remains to prove c). We have $\widetilde C=C\times_Z\widetilde Z$, where $\widetilde Z$ denotes, as above, the strict localization of $Z$ at $\bar z$. Put $\widetilde Y=Y\times_Z\widetilde Z$. Then there is a commutative diagram of restriction arrows
\[
\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=large,row sep=large]
\mathrm R\Gamma(Z-Y,i'_!L)=\mathrm R\Gamma(Z,Rj_*(i'_!L))
 \arrow[r,"{\hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:4.3.2]{4.3.2}}"] \arrow[d]
&
\mathrm R\Gamma(\widetilde C-\bar s,i'_!(L)|\widetilde C-\bar s)\\
\mathrm R\Gamma(Y,m^*Rj_*(i'_!L)) \arrow[r,"{\hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:4.3.1]{4.3.1}}"']
&
(Rj_*(i'_!L))_{\bar s}
  =\mathrm R\Gamma(\widetilde Z-\widetilde Y,(i'_!L)|\widetilde Z-\widetilde Y) \arrow[u]
\end{tikzcd}
\]
The vanishing of \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:4.3.2]{4.3.2} therefore follows from b), completing the proof of \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:lemma:4.3]{4.3}.

We prove \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:proposition:4.2]{4.2}. Let $f:T'\to T$ be the blowing-up of the maximal ideal of $T$, let $D=f^{-1}(z)(\simeq\mathbb P^1_S)$ be the exceptional divisor, and let $A'$, $B'$, $X'$ be the respective strict transforms of $A$, $B$, $X$. Then $T'$ is regular, connected, of dimension $2$, $X'$ is a regular divisor meeting $D$ transversely at one point $z'$, and the general-position hypotheses on $A$, $B$, $X$ mean that $A'$ is disjoint from $B'\cup X'$. Let $E'$ be the inverse image of $E$ on $T'-(A'\cup D\cup X')$, and let
\[
i':T'-(A'\cup D\cup X')\hookrightarrow T'-(A'\cup D)
\]
be the inclusion. We must prove that the restriction arrow
\begin{equation}
\mathrm R\Gamma(T'-(A'\cup D),i'_!(E'))
\longrightarrow
\mathrm R\Gamma(B'\cup D,i'_!(E')|B'-(B'\cup D))
\tag{*}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:unnumbered:4}
\end{equation}
is zero. But $B'$ is a disjoint union of strictly local schemes $B'_j$ $(1\le j\le n)$, of dimension $1$, such that for every $j$, $B'_j\cap D$ is reduced to a point $b_j$. Applying \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:lemma:4.3]{4.3 c)} to $(Z=T'-A', X=X', Y=D-(D\cap A'), L=E', C=B'_j)$, the hypothesis (P) being verified by virtue of the relative purity theorem (SGA 4 XVI 3), since $(Z,Y)$ is a filtered projective limit of smooth pairs of codimension $1$ over $S$, with affine étale transition morphisms, one obtains that the restriction arrow
\[
\mathrm R\Gamma(T'-(A'\cup D),i'_!(E'))
\longrightarrow
\mathrm R\Gamma(B'_j-b_j,i'_!(E')|B'_j-b_j)
\]
is zero. Therefore (*) is zero, which completes the proof of \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:proposition:4.2]{4.2}, hence of \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:proposition:2.5]{2.5} and \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:theorem:1.2]{1.2}.

\section*{II. Equivariant correspondences}

\subsection*{5. Non-commutative traces}\phantomsection\label{sga5:IIIB:subsection:5}

Throughout this number, fix a topos $T$ and a commutative unital ring $K$ of $T$. The rings under consideration will be assumed associative and unital, but not necessarily commutative. By a $K$-algebra we shall mean any (associative, unital) ring $A$ of $T$ endowed with a (unital) homomorphism $K\to A$ whose image is contained in the center.

\subsubsection*{5.0. Introduction}\phantomsection\label{sga5:IIIB:subsubsection:5.0}

Let $E$ be a $K$-module locally a direct factor of a free module of finite type. There is then a trace homomorphism, local in nature,
\begin{equation}\tag{5.0.1}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.0.1}
\Tr_K:\uHom_K(E,E)\longrightarrow K,
\end{equation}
defined by composing the evaluation arrow
\begin{equation}\tag{5.0.2}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.0.2}
\uHom_K(E,K)\otimes_KE\longrightarrow K,\qquad f\otimes x\longmapsto f(x),
\end{equation}
with the inverse of the canonical tensor-product isomorphism
\begin{equation}\tag{5.0.3}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.0.3}
\uHom_K(E,K)\otimes_KE\longrightarrow\uHom_K(E,E),\qquad f\otimes y\longmapsto (x\longmapsto f(x)y).
\end{equation}
The arrows \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.0.2]{5.0.2} and \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.0.3]{5.0.3} are defined more generally for every $K$-module $E$, and from them one obtains, for every complex of $K$-modules $E$, homomorphisms of complexes
\begin{align}
\uHom_K^\bullet(E,K)\otimes_KE&\longrightarrow K,\qquad f\otimes x\longmapsto f(x),\tag{5.0.4}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.0.4}\\
\uHom_K^\bullet(E,K)\otimes_KE&\longrightarrow \uHom_K^\bullet(E,E),\qquad f\otimes y\longmapsto (x\longmapsto f(x)y).\tag{5.0.5}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.0.5}
\end{align}
Upon deriving, \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.0.4]{5.0.4} and \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.0.5]{5.0.5} give, for $E\in\ob D^-(K)$, arrows in $D(K)$
\begin{align}
\uRHom_K(E,K)\lten_KE&\longrightarrow K,\tag{5.0.6}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.0.6}\\
\uRHom_K(E,K)\lten_KE&\longrightarrow\uRHom_K(E,E).\tag{5.0.7}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.0.7}
\end{align}
If $E$ is perfect (SGA 6 I), \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.0.7]{5.0.7} is an isomorphism, as is readily checked by reduction to the case $E=K$, and, by composing the inverse of \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.0.7]{5.0.7} with \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.0.6]{5.0.6}, one obtains a trace homomorphism, global in nature,
\begin{equation}\tag{5.0.8}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.0.8}
\Tr_K:\uRHom_K(E,E)\longrightarrow K,
\end{equation}
which generalizes \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.0.1]{5.0.1} (for more details, see SGA 6 I 7, 8).

We propose here to examine to what extent, in the preceding constructions, $K$ can be replaced by a $K$-algebra $A$ (we shall be interested chiefly in the case where $A$ is the $K$-algebra of an ordinary finite group), and in particular to define a trace arrow analogous to \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.0.8]{5.0.8}, generalizing the notion of non-commutative trace introduced by Stallings in [5] and developed by Grothendieck in Expos\'e XI of this seminar. As was said in the introduction, that expos\'e, written by I. Bucur, was unfortunately lost; the reader may nevertheless refer to Deligne's expos\'e (SGA $4^{1/2}$, Rapport), where the essential points are indicated. Although replacing $K$ by $A$ in \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.0.3]{5.0.3} presents no difficulty, the same is not true in \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.0.2]{5.0.2}: if $E$ is a left $A$-module, the evaluation arrow
\[
\uHom_A(E,A)\otimes_KE\longrightarrow A,
\qquad f\otimes x\longmapsto f(x),
\]
does not in general factor through $\uHom_A(E,A)\otimes_AE$, but, upon passage to the quotient, gives an arrow
\begin{equation}\tag{5.0.9}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.0.9}
\uHom_A(E,A)\otimes_AE\longrightarrow A_\natural,
\end{equation}
where $A_\natural$ is the quotient $K$-module of $A$ by the sub-$K$-module locally generated by local sections of the form $ab-ba$. If $E$ is locally a direct factor of a free left $A$-module of finite type, the tensor-product arrow, analogous to \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.0.3]{5.0.3},
\begin{equation}\tag{5.0.10}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.0.10}
\uHom_A(E,A)\otimes_AE\longrightarrow\uHom_A(E,E)
\end{equation}
is an isomorphism, and, by composing the inverse of \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.0.10]{5.0.10} with \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.0.9]{5.0.9}, one obtains a trace homomorphism
\begin{equation}\tag{5.0.11}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.0.11}
\Tr_A:\uHom_A(E,E)\longrightarrow A_\natural,
\end{equation}
which reduces to \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.0.1]{5.0.1} when $A=K$. One also checks readily (see \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:subsubsection:5.7]{5.7}) that, when $T$ is punctual, \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.0.11]{5.0.11} coincides with the trace homomorphism of Stallings and Grothendieck. In this number we shall examine the extension to complexes and the derivation of the arrows \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.0.9]{5.0.9}, \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.0.10]{5.0.10}, \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.0.11]{5.0.11}, and the behavior of the resulting arrows with respect to extension and restriction of scalars and to certain external tensor products. In fact, we shall derive arrows slightly more general than \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.0.9]{5.0.9} and \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.0.10]{5.0.10}, so that the constructions may be applied, in no.~6, to the definition of ``non-commutative'' Lefschetz--Verdier local terms.

\subsubsection*{5.1. Notation}\phantomsection\label{sga5:IIIB:subsubsection:5.1}

If $A$ is a $K$-algebra, we denote by $A^\circ$ the algebra opposite to $A$, and by $A^e=A\otimes_KA^\circ$ the enveloping algebra of $A$. Giving an $A$-bimodule, a left $A^e$-module, or a right $A^e$-module amounts to the same thing. In particular, $A$ is an $A$-bimodule, and hence both a left and a right $A^e$-module. If $E$ is a bimodule, recall ([1] IX 4) that
\[
H_0(A,E)=A\otimes_{A^e}E=E\otimes_{A^e}A
\]
is the quotient $K$-module of $E$ by the sub-$K$-module locally generated by local sections of the form $ax-xa$, for $x\in E$, $a\in A$\footnote{If $M$ is a sheaf, the notation $x\in M$ means that $x$ is a local section of $M$.}. We shall sometimes write $E_\natural$ instead of $H_0(A,E)$. In particular, $A_\natural$ is the $K$-module defined in \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.0.9]{5.0.9}. If $A=K[G]$, where $G$ is an ordinary finite group, $A_\natural$ is simply the free $K$-module on the set $G_\natural$ of conjugacy classes of $G$.

If $A$, $B$ are $K$-algebras, we shall use the Cartan--Eilenberg notation ${}_AE$, $E_B$, ${}_AE_B$ to denote respectively a left $A$-module, a right $B$-module, and a module that is left over $A$ and right over $B$. Finally, we shall denote by $D(A)$ (resp. $D(A,B)$) the derived category of the category of left $A$-modules (resp. of $A$-$B$-bimodules, left over $A$ and right over $B$).

\subsubsection*{5.2}\phantomsection\label{sga5:IIIB:subsubsection:5.2}

Let $A$, $B$ be $K$-algebras. Recall that, in the situation $({}_AE,{}_BF_A,{}_BG)$, there is a canonical isomorphism
\begin{equation}\tag{5.2.1}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.2.1}
\uHom_A(E,\uHom_B(F,G))\xrightarrow{\sim}\uHom_B(F\otimes_AE,G),\qquad
f\longmapsto (y\otimes x\longmapsto f(x)(y)).
\end{equation}
This derives to an isomorphism
\begin{equation}\tag{5.2.2}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.2.2}
\uRHom_A(E,\uRHom_B(F,G))\xrightarrow{\sim}\uRHom_B(F\lten_AE,G)
\end{equation}
for $E\in\ob D^-(A)$, $F\in\ob D(B,A)$, $G\in\ob D^+(B)$. Indeed, one may suppose that $E$ is bounded above with flat terms and that $G$ is bounded below with injective terms; one then has $\uRHom_B(F\lten_AE,G)=\Hom_B^\bullet(F\otimes_AE,G)$, $\uRHom_B(F,G)=\Hom_B^\bullet(F,G)$, and $\uRHom_A(E,\Hom_B^\bullet(F,G))=\Hom_A^\bullet(E,\Hom_B^\bullet(F,G))$, since, if $E$ is acyclic, $F\otimes_AE$ is acyclic, and hence $\Hom_A^\bullet(E,\Hom_B^\bullet(F,G))\simeq\Hom_B^\bullet(F\otimes_AE,G)$ is acyclic. For $E$ and $G$ as above, \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.2.2]{5.2.2} is given by the isomorphism of complexes
\[
\Hom_A^\bullet(E,\Hom_B^\bullet(F,G))\xrightarrow{\sim}\Hom_B^\bullet(F\otimes_AE,G)
\]
defined by \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.2.1]{5.2.1}. Note that, if $A$ and $B$ are flat over $K$, \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.2.2]{5.2.2} still holds under the conditions $E\in\ob D(A)$, $F\in\ob D^-(B,A)$, $G\in\ob D^+(B)$, as is seen by resolving $F$ by flat $(B\otimes_KA^\circ)$-modules. From \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.2.2]{5.2.2} one deduces an isomorphism
\begin{equation}\tag{5.2.3}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.2.3}
\uHom_A(E,\uRHom_B(F,G))\xrightarrow{\sim}\uHom_B(F\lten_AE,G).
\end{equation}

\subsubsection*{5.3}\phantomsection\label{sga5:IIIB:subsubsection:5.3}

Let $A$ be a $K$-algebra. In the situation $(E_A,{}_AF)$, there is a canonical isomorphism
\begin{equation}\tag{5.3.1}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.3.1}
E\otimes_AF\xrightarrow{\sim}(E\otimes_KF)\otimes_{A^e}A,
\qquad x\otimes y\longmapsto (x\otimes y)\otimes 1.
\end{equation}
On the other hand, in the situation $({}_AE,{}_AF_A)$, the evaluation arrow
\begin{equation}\tag{5.3.2}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.3.2}
\uHom_A(E,F)\otimes_KE\longrightarrow F,
\qquad f\otimes x\longmapsto f(x)
\end{equation}
is $A^e$-linear, and therefore induces, via \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.3.1]{5.3.1}, upon applying $-\otimes_{A^e}A$, an arrow
\begin{equation}\tag{5.3.3}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.3.3}
\uHom_A(E,F)\otimes_AE\longrightarrow F\otimes_{A^e}A\;(=F_\natural).
\end{equation}
In other words, the composite of \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.3.2]{5.3.2} and the canonical arrow $F\to F_\natural$, $x\mapsto x\otimes 1$, factors uniquely through \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.3.3]{5.3.3}.

Suppose that $A$ is flat over $K$. Then \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.3.1]{5.3.1} derives to an isomorphism
\begin{equation}\tag{5.3.4}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.3.4}
E\lten_AF\xrightarrow{\sim}(E\lten_KF)\lten_{A^e}A
\end{equation}
for $E\in\ob D^-(A^\circ)$, $F\in\ob D^-(A)$ (resolve $E$ and $F$ by flat modules). On the other hand, \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.3.3]{5.3.3} derives to an arrow of $D(K)$, called the evaluation arrow,
\begin{equation}\tag{5.3.5}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.3.5}
\uRHom_A(E,F)\lten_AE\longrightarrow F\lten_{A^e}A,
\end{equation}
for $E\in\ob D^-(A)$, $F\in\ob D^b(A^e)$ such that $\uRHom_A(E,F)\in\ob D^b(A^\circ)$. One first derives \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.3.2]{5.3.2} to an arrow of $D^-((A^e)^\circ)$,
\begin{equation}\tag{5.3.6}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.3.6}
\uRHom_A(E,F)\lten_KE\longrightarrow F,
\end{equation}
by resolving $E$ by flat modules and $F$ by injective $A^e$-modules, and then one applies the functor $-\lten_{A^e}A$ to \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.3.6]{5.3.6}, taking \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.3.4]{5.3.4} into account.

\subsubsection*{5.4}\phantomsection\label{sga5:IIIB:subsubsection:5.4}

Let $A$ be a $K$-algebra. In the situation $({}_AE,{}_AF_A,{}_AG)$, there is a canonical arrow
\begin{equation}\tag{5.4.1}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.4.1}
\uHom_A(E,F)\otimes_AG\longrightarrow\uHom_A(E,F\otimes_AG),
\qquad u\otimes y\longmapsto(x\longmapsto u(x)\otimes y),
\end{equation}
which corresponds by adjunction \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.2.1]{(5.2.1)} to the $A$-linear arrow
\[
E\otimes_K\uHom_A(E,F)\otimes_AG\longrightarrow F\otimes_AG
\]
defined by the evaluation arrow $E\otimes_K\uHom_A(E,F)\to F$. The arrow \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.4.1]{5.4.1} derives to an arrow of $D(K)$, called the tensor-product arrow,
\begin{equation}\tag{5.4.2}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.4.2}
\uRHom_A(E,F)\lten_AG\longrightarrow\uRHom_A(E,F\lten_AG),
\end{equation}
for $E\in\ob D^-(A)$, $F\in\ob D^+(A^e)$, $G\in\ob D^-(A)$, with $F\lten_AG\in\ob D^+(A)$. If $E$ and $G$ are bounded above with flat terms, and $F$ is bounded below with injective terms, the first member of \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.4.2]{5.4.2} identifies with $\Hom_A^\bullet(E,F)\otimes_AG$, and it is sent to the second member by the composite of \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.4.1]{5.4.1} and the natural arrow
\[
\Hom_A^\bullet(E,F\otimes_AG)\longrightarrow \uRHom_A(E,F\otimes_AG)=\uRHom_A(E,F\lten_AG).
\]
The arrow \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.4.2]{5.4.2} is not an isomorphism in general. It is, however, an isomorphism in certain important cases, for example if $E$ is perfect (as is checked by reducing to $E=A$). We shall see another interesting example later \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:6.2.3]{(6.2.3)}.

The arrows \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.3.5]{5.3.5} and \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.4.2]{5.4.2} will play an essential role throughout the rest of the expos\'e. The examples of objects $F$ that we have in mind are: a) $F=A$, or $F=A$ regarded as a right $A$-module in the natural way and as a left $A$-module through a homomorphism of $K$-algebras $\varphi:A\to A$ (cf. \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:subsubsection:5.13]{5.13}); b) $F=M\lten_KA$, with $M\in\ob D^+(K)$; in no.~6, $M$ will be a dualizing complex, see especially \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:6.3.2]{6.3.2}, \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:6.4.1]{6.4.1}, \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:6.5.6]{6.5.6}.

\subsubsection*{5.5}\phantomsection\label{sga5:IIIB:subsubsection:5.5}

Denote by $DF(A)$ the derived category of complexes of left $A$-modules endowed with a finite filtration ([3] V). Then, in the situation of \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:subsubsection:5.2]{5.2}, there is a canonical isomorphism in $DF(K)$, analogous to \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.2.2]{5.2.2}, for $E\in\ob D^-F(A)$, $F\in\ob D^-F(B,A)$ ($=D^-F(B\otimes_KA^\circ)$), $G\in\ob D^+F(B)$. Similarly, there are ``filtered derived'' arrows analogous to \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.3.4]{5.3.4}, \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.3.5]{5.3.5}, \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.3.6]{5.3.6}, \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.4.2]{5.4.2}. These arrows are compatible with passage to associated graded objects, in the sense of ([3] V 1,2).

\subsubsection*{5.6}\phantomsection\label{sga5:IIIB:subsubsection:5.6}

Assume that $A$ is flat over $K$. Denote by $D(A)_{\mathrm{parf}}$ the full subcategory of $D(A)$ formed by complexes of finite perfect amplitude, that is, perfect complexes of finite tor-dimension (SGA 6 I). We define a canonical arrow, called the trace,
\begin{equation}\tag{5.6.1}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.6.1}
\Tr_A=\uRHom_A(E,F\lten_AE)\longrightarrow F\lten_{A^e}A
\end{equation}
for $E\in\ob D(A)_{\mathrm{parf}}$, $F\in\ob D^b(A^e)$, by composing the inverse isomorphism of \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.4.2]{5.4.2} for $G=E$ with the evaluation arrow \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.3.5]{5.3.5}. From this one deduces a trace arrow
\begin{equation}\tag{5.6.2}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.6.2}
\Tr_A:\uHom_A(E,F\lten_AE)\longrightarrow H^0(T,F\lten_{A^e}A).
\end{equation}
For $F=A$, by composing with the canonical arrow $A\lten_{A^e}A\to A\otimes_{A^e}A=A_\natural$ \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:subsubsection:5.1]{(5.1)}, one obtains arrows, again called traces,
\begin{align}
\Tr_A:\uRHom_A(E,E)&\longrightarrow A_\natural,\tag{5.6.3}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.6.3}\\
\Tr_A:\uHom_A(E,E)&\longrightarrow H^0(T,A_\natural).\tag{5.6.4}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.6.4}
\end{align}
Notice that \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.6.4]{5.6.4} is a rather innocuous arrow, local in nature, whereas \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.6.3]{5.6.3} is a ``serious'' arrow, global in nature.

Denote by $DF(A)_{\mathrm{parf}}$ the full subcategory of $DF(A)$ formed by filtered complexes whose associated graded object has finite perfect amplitude. Then there is an arrow in $DF(K)$ analogous to \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.6.1]{5.6.1} for $E\in\ob DF(A)_{\mathrm{parf}}$, $F\in\ob D^bF(A^e)$, whence arrows analogous to \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.6.2]{5.6.2}, \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.6.3]{5.6.3}, \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.6.4]{5.6.4}. It follows from the remarks in \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:subsubsection:5.5]{5.5} that the filtered arrow \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.6.1]{5.6.1} is compatible with passage to the associated graded object (cf. [3] V 3.7.2), and one deduces, as in loc. cit., that, if the filtration on $F$ is concentrated in degree $0$, then, for $u\in\Hom_{DF(A)}(E,F\lten_AE)$, one has
\begin{equation}\tag{5.6.5}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.6.5}
\Tr_A(u)=\sum_i\Tr_A(\operatorname{gr}^i u).
\end{equation}
This formula implies in particular that, for $E\in\ob D(A)_{\mathrm{parf}}$, $F\in\ob D^b(A^e)$, $u\in\uHom_A(E,F\lten_AE)$, one has
\begin{equation}\tag{5.6.6}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.6.6}
\Tr(u[1])=-\Tr(u),
\end{equation}
where $u[1]:E[1]\to(F\lten_AE)[1]$ is the translate of $u$.

\subsubsection*{5.7}\phantomsection\label{sga5:IIIB:subsubsection:5.7}

The traces defined in \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:subsubsection:5.6]{5.6} simultaneously generalize the usual commutative traces ((SGA 6 I), ([3] V 3)) and the non-commutative traces of Stallings [5] and Grothendieck.

(a) Suppose that $A=K$. Then $A^e=A_\natural=K$, the arrow \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.6.1]{5.6.1} is written
\[
\Tr_K:\uRHom_K(E,F\lten_KE)\longrightarrow F
\]
and coincides with the trace arrow defined in ([3] V 3.7.1), in the special case where the complex $K$ of loc. cit. is reduced to $A$. In particular,
\[
\Tr_K:\uRHom_K(E,E)\longrightarrow K
\]
coincides with the trace defined in (SGA 6 I 8).

(b) Assume again that $A$ is a flat $K$-algebra, not necessarily commutative. We propose to make \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.6.4]{5.6.4} explicit for $E$ strictly perfect, that is, bounded with terms locally direct factors of free modules of finite type. First, take $E=A$; $\uRHom_A(A,A)=\uHom_A(A,A)$ identifies with $A$ by $f\mapsto f(1)$, $a\mapsto(x\mapsto xa)$, and \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.6.3]{5.6.3} identifies with the canonical projection $A\to A_\natural$, which we shall denote by $a\mapsto a_\natural$; thus, if $d_a$ denotes right multiplication by $a$,
\begin{equation}\tag{5.7.1}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.7.1}
\Tr_A(d_a)=a_\natural.
\end{equation}
For $1\le i\le n$, let $E_i$ be a copy of $A$, and let $E=\bigoplus_{1\le i\le n}E_i$. The isomorphism \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.4.2]{5.4.2}, for $F=A$, $G=E$, is written
\[
\bigoplus_{1\le i,j\le n}\uHom_A(E_i,A)\otimes_AE_j\longrightarrow\uHom_A(E,E),
\]
and, for $u=\sum u_{ij}\otimes 1\in\uHom_A(E,E)$ $(u_{ij}\otimes 1\in\uHom_A(E_i,A)\otimes_A E_j)$, one has
\begin{equation}\tag{5.7.2}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.7.2}
\Tr_A(u)=\sum_i (u_{ii})_\natural,
\end{equation}
by definition of the evaluation arrow \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.3.5]{5.3.5}. Now take for $E$ a direct factor of $A^n$, the image of a projector $p$, and denote by $i:E\to A^n$ the inclusion; then, for $u\in\uHom_A(E,E)$, one has
\begin{equation}\tag{5.7.3}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.7.3}
\Tr_A(u)=\Tr_A(iup),
\end{equation}
as is seen either with the aid of \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.6.5]{5.6.5} or directly from the definition. Finally, suppose that $E$ is strictly perfect; if $u\in\Hom_A(E,E)$ is a homomorphism of complexes, one has, by \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.6.5]{5.6.5} and \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.6.6]{5.6.6},
\begin{equation}\tag{5.7.4}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.7.4}
\Tr_A(u)=\sum_i(-1)^i\Tr_A(u^i).
\end{equation}
The fact that \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.6.4]{5.6.4} is obtained, by applying $H^0$, from a homomorphism of complexes $\uHom_A^\bullet(E,E)\to A$ \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.6.3]{(5.6.3)} implies that, if $u$, $v\in\Hom_A(E,E)$ are homotopic, then
\begin{equation}\tag{5.7.5}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.7.5}
\Tr_A(u)=\Tr_A(v).
\end{equation}
Since every endomorphism of $E$ in $D(A)$ is locally defined by an endomorphism of complexes, locally unique up to homotopy (SGA 6 I 4.10), the \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.7.2]{formulas 5.7.2} to \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.7.4]{5.7.4} therefore permit the calculation of $\Tr_A(u)$ for every endomorphism $u$ of $E$ in $D(A)$ and, combined with \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.7.5]{5.7.5}, provide a direct definition of $\Tr_A(u)$. When the topos $T$ is punctual, this recovers the definition of (SGA $4^{1/2}$, Rapport 4.3).

\subsubsection*{5.8}\phantomsection\label{sga5:IIIB:subsubsection:5.8}

Let $P,Q$ be $A$-bimodules. Then $P\otimes_AQ$ and $Q\otimes_AP$ are $A$-bimodules, and there are canonical isomorphisms
\begin{equation}\tag{5.8.1}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.8.1}
(P\otimes_AQ)\otimes_{A^e}A\xrightarrow{\sim}P\otimes_{A^e}Q\xrightarrow{\sim}(Q\otimes_AP)\otimes_{A^e}A\xrightarrow{\sim}A\otimes_{A^e}(P\otimes_KQ)\otimes_{A^e}A,
\end{equation}
each of the objects above identifying canonically with the quotient $K$-module of $P\otimes_KQ$ by the submodule generated by sections of the form $axb\otimes y-x\otimes bya$, for $x\in P$, $y\in Q$, $a,b\in A$. For $A$ flat over $K$, these isomorphisms derive to isomorphisms
\begin{equation}\tag{5.8.2}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.8.2}
(P\lten_AQ)\lten_{A^e}A\simeq P\lten_{A^e}Q\simeq(Q\lten_AP)\lten_{A^e}A\simeq A\lten_{A^e}(P\lten_KQ)\lten_{A^e}A
\end{equation}
for $P,Q\in\ob D^-(A^e)$. On the other hand, the composition arrow at the level of modules
\[
\uHom_A(E,P\otimes_AF)\otimes_K\uHom_A(F,Q\otimes_AE)
\longrightarrow\uHom_A(E,P\otimes_AQ\otimes_AE),
\]
\[
f\otimes g\longmapsto (P\otimes_Ag)f,
\]
derives to
\begin{equation}\tag{5.8.3}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.8.3}
\uRHom_A(E,P\lten_AF)\lten_K\uRHom_A(F,Q\lten_AE)
\longrightarrow\uRHom_A(E,P\lten_AQ\lten_AE)
\end{equation}
for $P,Q\in\ob D^b(A^e)$, $E,F\in\ob D^b(A)$ such that the tensor products and $\uRHom$'s appearing above belong to $D^b$. When $E$ and $F$ are perfect, this arrow identifies, via \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.4.2]{5.4.2}, with the arrow deduced from the evaluation arrow $F\lten_K\uRHom_A(F,Q)\to Q$; from this one readily deduces that there is a commutative square
\begin{equation}\tag{5.8.4}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.8.4}
\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=large,row sep=large]
\uRHom_A(E,P\lten_AF)\lten_K\uRHom_A(F,Q\lten_AE) \arrow[r,"(1)"] \arrow[d,"(2)"'] & \uRHom_A(E,P\lten_AQ\lten_AE) \arrow[d,"\Tr_A"]\\
\uRHom_A(F,Q\lten_AP\lten_AF) \arrow[r,"\Tr_A"'] & P\lten_{A^e}Q,
\end{tikzcd}
\end{equation}
where (1) and (2) are given by \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.8.3]{5.8.3}. The point is that the two composite arrows in \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.8.4]{5.8.4} are defined by the tensor product of the evaluation arrows
\[
F\lten_K\uRHom_A(F,Q)\longrightarrow Q,\qquad
E\lten_K\uRHom_A(E,P)\longrightarrow P.
\]
It follows that, for $u\in\uHom_A(E,P\lten_AF)$, $v\in\uHom_A(F,Q\lten_AE)$, one has
\begin{equation}\tag{5.8.5}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.8.5}
\Tr_A(vu)=\Tr_A(uv).
\end{equation}
We shall sometimes denote the common value in \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.8.5]{5.8.5} by $\langle u,v\rangle_A$.

\subsubsection*{5.9. Traces and extension of scalars}\phantomsection\label{sga5:IIIB:subsubsection:5.9}

Let $A\to B$ be a morphism of flat $K$-algebras. It induces a morphism of $K$-algebras $A^e\to B^e$, hence an extension-of-scalars functor $-\lten_{A^e}B^e:D(A^e)\longrightarrow D(B^e)$.
For $E,F$ as in \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.3.5]{5.3.5}, if $F\lten_{A^e}B^e\in\ob D^b(B^e)$ and $\RHom_B(B\lten_AE,F\lten_{A^e}B^e)\in\ob D^b((B^e)^\circ)$, there is a commutative square in $D(K)$
\begin{equation}\tag{5.9.1}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.9.1}
\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=large,row sep=large]
\RHom_A(E,F)\lten_{A^e}E \arrow[r,"(1)"] \arrow[d,"(3)"'] & F\lten_{A^e}A \arrow[d,"(4)"]\\
\RHom_B(B\lten_AE,F\lten_{A^e}B^e)\lten_{B^e}(B\lten_AE) \arrow[r,"(2)"'] & (F\lten_{A^e}B^e)\lten_{B^e}B,
\end{tikzcd}
\end{equation}
where the arrows (1) and (2) are defined by \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.3.6]{5.3.6}, (4) by $A\to B$, and (3) by the canonical arrow $F\to F\lten_{A^e}B^e$. The verification is immediate. Similarly, for $E,F,G$ as in \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.4.2]{5.4.2}, if $F\lten_{A^e}B^e\in\ob D^+(B^e)$ and $(F\lten_{A^e}B^e)\lten_B(B\lten_AG)\in\ob D^+(B)$, one has a commutative square in $D(K)$
\begin{equation}\tag{5.9.2}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.9.2}
\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=large,row sep=large]
\RHom_A(E,F)\lten_AG \arrow[r,"(1)"] \arrow[d,"(3)"'] & \RHom_A(E,F\lten_AG) \arrow[d,"(4)"]\\
\RHom_B(B\lten_AE,F\lten_{A^e}B^e)\lten_B(B\lten_AG) \arrow[r,"(2)"'] & \RHom_B(B\lten_AE,(F\lten_{A^e}B^e)\lten_B(B\lten_AG)),
\end{tikzcd}
\end{equation}
where (1) and (2) are defined by \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.4.2]{5.4.2}, and (3) and (4) by $F\to F\lten_{A^e}B^e$. From this one deduces, for $E,F$ as in \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.6.1]{5.6.1}, with $F\lten_{A^e}B^e\in\ob D^b(B^e)$, a commutative square in $D(K)$, analogous to the preceding ones, in which the vertical arrows are defined by \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.6.1]{5.6.1}:
\begin{equation}\tag{5.9.3}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.9.3}
\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=large,row sep=large]
\RHom_A(E,F\lten_AE) \arrow[r,"\Tr_A"] \arrow[d] & F\lten_{A^e}A \arrow[d]\\
\RHom_B(B\lten_AE,(F\lten_{A^e}B^e)\lten_B(B\lten_AE)) \arrow[r,"\Tr_B"'] & (F\lten_{A^e}B^e)\lten_{B^e}B.
\end{tikzcd}
\end{equation}
Finally, if one is given $F'\in\ob D^b(B^e)$ such that $\RHom_A(E,F')\in\ob D^b((B^\circ))$, and $f:F\lten_{A^e}B^e\to F'$, then there is a compatibility slightly more general than \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.9.1]{5.9.1}, expressed by an analogous commutative square, with $F'$ in place of $F\lten_{A^e}B^e$ in the lower row and with the vertical arrows defined by $f$. One has similarly commutative squares analogous to \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.9.2]{5.9.2} and \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.9.3]{5.9.3} with $F'$ in place of $F\lten_{A^e}B^e$. Taking in particular $F=A$ and, for $f:A\lten_{A^e}B^e\to B$, the arrow defined by $A\to B$, one obtains a commutative square
\begin{equation}\tag{5.9.4}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.9.4}
\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=large,row sep=large]
\RHom_A(E,E) \arrow[r,"\Tr_A"] \arrow[d] & A\lten_{A^e}A \arrow[d]\\
\RHom_B(B\lten_AE,B\lten_AE) \arrow[r,"\Tr_B"'] & B\lten_{B^e}B,
\end{tikzcd}
\end{equation}
where the left vertical arrow, respectively the right vertical arrow, is given by extension of scalars, respectively is the canonical arrow deduced from $A\to B$.

\subsubsection*{5.10. Traces and restriction of scalars}\phantomsection\label{sga5:IIIB:subsubsection:5.10}

Let $A\to B$ be a morphism of flat $K$-algebras such that $B$, as a left $A$-module, is flat and of finite presentation, i.e. locally a direct factor of a free $A$-module of finite type. This is the case for example if $A\to B$ is the morphism $K[H]\to K[G]$ defined by an injective morphism of discrete groups $H\hookrightarrow G$ of finite index. The scalar-restriction homomorphism
\[
\Hom_B(B,B)\longrightarrow\Hom_A(B,B),
\]
where $B$ is considered as a left module, identifies, by the canonical isomorphisms
\[
B\xrightarrow{\sim}\Hom_B(B,B),\qquad \Hom_A(B,A)\otimes_AB\xrightarrow{\sim}\Hom_A(B,B),
\]
with an arrow of $B$-bimodules
\begin{equation}\tag{5.10.1}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.10.1}
B\longrightarrow\Hom_A(B,A)\otimes_AB.
\end{equation}
The composite of \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.10.1]{5.10.1} with the evaluation arrow $\Hom_A(B,A)\otimes_AB\to A_\natural$ \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.3.3]{(5.3.3)} is the homomorphism $f\mapsto\Tr_A(f)$ ($=\Tr_A(d_f)$, where $d_f$ is right multiplication by $f$); hence, by the formula $\Tr_A(f_g)=\Tr_A(gf)$ \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.8.5]{(5.8.5)}, it factors through $B_\natural$ as an arrow again denoted $\Tr_{B/A}:B_\natural\to A_\natural$. Thus there is a commutative square
\begin{equation}\tag{5.10.2}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.10.2}
\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=large,row sep=large]
B \arrow[r,"\Tr_B"] \arrow[d,"{\hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.10.1]{5.10.1}}"'] & B_\natural \arrow[d,"\Tr_{B/A}"]\\
\Hom_A(B,A)\otimes_AB \arrow[r,"\mathrm{ev}"'] & A_\natural .
\end{tikzcd}
\end{equation}
In the situation $({}_BE,{}^BP_A)$, one has a homomorphism of right $B$-modules
\begin{equation}\tag{5.10.3}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.10.3}
\Hom_B(E,P)\otimes_AB\longrightarrow\Hom_B(E,P\otimes_AB),\qquad
u\otimes b\longmapsto(x\longmapsto u(x)\otimes b),
\end{equation}
which is an isomorphism, as is checked immediately: it is enough to see that the underlying $K$-linear homomorphism is an isomorphism, and this follows from the fact that $B$ is locally a direct factor of a free $A$-module of finite type. This isomorphism derives to an isomorphism of $D(B)$
\begin{equation}\tag{5.10.4}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.10.4}
\RHom_B(E,P)\lten_AB\xrightarrow{\sim}\RHom_B(E,P\lten_AB),
\end{equation}
for $E\in\ob D^-(B)$, $P\in\ob D^+(B\otimes_KA^\circ)$: resolve $E$ by modules of the form $B\otimes_AL$, where $L$ is flat, and $P$ by injective $(B\otimes_KA^\circ)$-modules, which are necessarily injective as $B$-modules, since $A$ is flat over $K$; this allows one to remove the $R$ on the left, and the resolution chosen for $E$ also allows one to remove it on the right.

For $E\in\ob D^-(B)$, $M\in\ob D^+(K)$, $F\in\ob D^-(B)$, define an arrow in $D(K)$\footnote{In no.~6 we shall apply the constructions that follow to the case where $M$ is a dualizing complex \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:subsection:6.7]{(6.7)}.}
\begin{equation}\tag{5.10.5}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.10.5}
\RHom_B(E,B\lten_KM)\lten_BF\longrightarrow\RHom_A(E,A\lten_KM)\lten_AF
\end{equation}
as the composite of the arrows
\[
\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=small]
\RHom_B(E,B\lten_KM)\lten_BF \arrow[r,"(1)"] & \RHom_B(E,\Hom_A(B,A)\lten_A(B\lten_KM))\lten_BF \\
\arrow[r,"(2)"] & \RHom_B(E,\Hom_A(B,A)\lten_KM)\lten_A B\lten_BF \\
\arrow[r,"(3)"] & \RHom_B(E,\Hom_A(B,A\lten_KM))\lten_AF \\
\arrow[r,"(4)"] & \RHom_A(E,A\lten_KM)\lten_AF,
\end{tikzcd}
\]
where (1) is defined by \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.10.1]{5.10.1}, (2) by the inverse isomorphism of \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.10.4]{5.10.4} with $P=\Hom_A(B,A)\lten_KM$, (3) by the canonical isomorphism
\[
\Hom_A(B,A)\lten_KM\xrightarrow{\sim}\Hom_A(B,A\lten_KM)
\]
\hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.4.2]{(5.4.2)}, and (4) by the adjunction isomorphism \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.2.2]{5.2.2}.

\phantomsection\label{sga5:IIIB:proposition:5.10.6}\begin{statement}{Proposition 5.10.6}
Suppose that $M\lten_KF\in\ob D^+(B)$. Then there is a commutative square in $D(K)$
\begin{equation}\tag{5.10.7}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.10.7}
\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=large,row sep=large]
\RHom_B(E,B\lten_KM)\lten_BF \arrow[r,"{\hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.4.2]{5.4.2}}"] \arrow[d,"{\hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.10.5]{5.10.5}}"'] & \RHom_B(E,M\lten_KF) \arrow[d,"\mathrm{res}"]\\
\RHom_A(E,A\lten_KM)\lten_AF \arrow[r,"{\hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.4.2]{5.4.2}}"'] & \RHom_A(E,M\lten_KF),
\end{tikzcd}
\end{equation}
where the right vertical arrow is restriction of scalars.
\end{statement}

One has to verify that there is a commutative square
\[
\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=large,row sep=large]
E\lten_K\RHom_B(E,B\lten_KM)\lten_BF \arrow[r] \arrow[d] & M\lten_KF \arrow[d,"\mathrm{res}"]\\
E\lten_K\RHom_A(E,A\lten_KM)\lten_AF \arrow[r] & M\lten_KF,
\end{tikzcd}
\]
where the horizontal arrows are given by the evaluations \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.3.6]{(5.3.6)}. This square is deduced, by applying $-\lten_BF$, from the analogous square with $F=B$, so that one may suppose $F=B$. The commutativity of \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.10.7]{5.10.7} in this case is equivalent to that of the triangle
\[
\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=huge,row sep=large]
\RHom_B(E,B\lten_KM) \arrow[d,"(1)"'] \arrow[dr,"(2)"] & \\
\RHom_B(E,\Hom_A(B,B\lten_KM)) \arrow[r,"(3)"'] & \RHom_A(E,B\lten_KM),
\end{tikzcd}
\]
where (1) is defined by \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.10.1]{5.10.1}, (2) is restriction of scalars, and (3) is the adjunction isomorphism. But the arrow $B\lten_KM\to\Hom_A(B,B\lten_KM)$ given by \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.10.1]{5.10.1} is nothing other than the adjunction arrow corresponding to the functors restriction of scalars and $\Hom_A(B,-)$; the commutativity follows at once.

Let $E\in\ob D^-(B)$, $M\in\ob D^b(K)$ be such that $\RHom_B(E,B\lten_KM)\in\ob D^b(B^\circ)$. Then, by \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.3.5]{5.3.5}, there is an evaluation arrow
\[
\RHom_B(E,B\lten_KM)\lten_{B^e}E\longrightarrow M\lten_K(B\lten_{B^e}B),
\]
which, by composition with the canonical arrow $B\lten_{B^e}B\to B_\natural$, gives an arrow
\begin{equation}\tag{5.10.8}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.10.8}
\RHom_B(E,B\lten_KM)\lten_BE\longrightarrow M\lten_KB_\natural.
\end{equation}

\phantomsection\label{sga5:IIIB:proposition:5.10.9}\begin{statement}{Proposition 5.10.9}
Suppose that $A$ is of finite presentation and flat as a $K$-module, and that $A_\natural$, $B_\natural$ are flat over $K$. Let $E\in\ob D^-(B)$, $M\in\ob D^b(K)$ be such that $\RHom_B(E,B\lten_KM)\in\ob D^b(B^\circ)$. Then $\RHom_A(E,A\lten_KM)\in\ob D^b(A^\circ)$, and there is a commutative square
\begin{equation}\tag{5.10.10}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.10.10}
\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=large,row sep=large]
\RHom_B(E,B\lten_KM)\lten_BE \arrow[r,"{\hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.10.8]{5.10.8}}"] \arrow[d,"{\hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.10.5]{5.10.5}}"'] & M\lten_KB_\natural \arrow[d,"M\otimes_K\Tr_{B/A}"]\\
\RHom_A(E,A\lten_KM)\lten_AE \arrow[r,"{\hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.10.8]{5.10.8}}"'] & M\lten_KA_\natural .
\end{tikzcd}
\end{equation}
\end{statement}

The additional hypotheses on $A$ and $B$ (verified, for example, if $A$ and $B$ are $K$-algebras of finite groups) are no doubt useless, and one should have a commutative square analogous to \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.10.10]{5.10.10} with $A_\natural$ (resp. $B_\natural$) replaced by $A\lten_{A^e}A$ (resp. $B\lten_{B^e}B$).

The first assertion of \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:proposition:5.10.9]{5.10.9} is trivial. We prove the second. We may suppose that $M$ has injective components and that $E$ has components of the form $B\otimes_KL$, with $L$ flat. Then
\[
\RHom_B(E,B\lten_KM)\lten_BE=\Hom_B^\bullet(E,B\otimes_KM)\otimes_BE,
\]
\[
\RHom_A(E,A\lten_KM)\lten_AE=\Hom_A^\bullet(E,A\otimes_KM)\otimes_AE,
\]
and the arrows in \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.10.10]{5.10.10} are defined at the level of complexes. Thus we are reduced to verifying that, if $L,M$ are $K$-modules and $E$ a left $B$-module, there is a commutative square
\[
\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=large,row sep=large]
|[alias=star]| {(*)} & \Hom_B(B\otimes_KL,B\otimes_KM)\otimes_B(B\otimes_KL) \arrow[r,"{\hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.3.3]{5.3.3}}"] \arrow[d,"(1)"'] & M\otimes_KB_\natural \arrow[d,"M\otimes_K\Tr_{B/A}"]\\
& \Hom_A(B\otimes_KL,A\otimes_KM)\otimes_A(B\otimes_KL) \arrow[r,"{\hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.3.3]{5.3.3}}"'] & M\otimes_KA_\natural,
\end{tikzcd}
\]
where the arrow (1) is defined by sending $B\otimes_KM$ into $\Hom_A(B,A)\otimes_AB\otimes_KM$ by \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.10.1]{5.10.1} and composing with an isomorphism of the type \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.10.3]{5.10.3} and an adjunction isomorphism. Since $A$ is flat and of finite presentation as a $K$-module, the ``tensor-product'' arrows
\[
\text{(2)}\quad B\otimes_K\Hom_K(L,M)=\Hom_B(B,B)\otimes_K\Hom_K(L,M)\to\Hom_B(B\otimes_KL,B\otimes_KM),
\]
\[
\text{(3)}\quad \Hom_A(B,A)\otimes_K\Hom_K(L,M)\to\Hom_A(B\otimes_KL,A\otimes_KM)
\]
are isomorphisms, by which (1) identifies with the tensor product (over $K$) of \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.10.1]{5.10.1} by $\Hom_K(L,M)\otimes_KL$. On the other hand, the upper (resp. lower) horizontal arrow of (*) identifies by (2) (resp. (3)) with the tensor product (over $K$) of $\Tr_B:B\to B_\natural$ (resp. $\mathrm{ev}:\Hom_A(B,A)\otimes_AB\to A_\natural$) by the evaluation arrow $\Hom_K(L,M)\otimes_KL\to M$. The commutativity therefore follows from that of \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.10.2]{5.10.2}, completing the proof of \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:proposition:5.10.9]{5.10.9}.

\phantomsection\label{sga5:IIIB:corollary:5.10.11}\begin{statement}{Corollary 5.10.11}
Under the hypotheses of \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:proposition:5.10.9]{5.10.9}, for $E\in\ob D(B)_{\mathrm{parf}}$, $M\in\ob D^b(K)$, there is a commutative square in $D(K)$
\begin{equation}\tag{5.10.12}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.10.12}
\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=large,row sep=large]
\RHom_B(E,M\lten_KE) \arrow[r,"\Tr_B"] \arrow[d] & M\lten_KB_\natural \arrow[d,"M\lten\Tr_{B/A}"]\\
\RHom_A(E,M\lten_KE) \arrow[r,"\Tr_A"'] & M\lten_KA_\natural,
\end{tikzcd}
\end{equation}
where the left vertical arrow is restriction of scalars.
\end{statement}

It is enough indeed to conjugate \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:proposition:5.10.6]{5.10.6} and \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:proposition:5.10.9]{5.10.9}.

\subsubsection*{5.11. Application to finite groups: divisibility properties}\phantomsection\label{sga5:IIIB:subsubsection:5.11}

Let $G$ be a finite group, with neutral element denoted $e$, and put $K[G]=B$. The $K$-module $B_\natural$ is free with basis the set $G_\natural$ of conjugacy classes of $G$; every section $s$ of $B_\natural$ is written uniquely
\[
s=\sum_{x\in G_\natural}s(x)x.
\]

\phantomsection\label{sga5:IIIB:proposition:5.11.1}\begin{statement}{Proposition 5.11.1}
Cf. SGA $4^{1/2}$, Rapport 4.5. Let $E\in\ob D(B)_{\mathrm{parf}}$, $M\in\ob D^b(K)$. For $u\in\Hom_B(E,M\lten_KE)$, one has, in $H^0(T,M)$,
\begin{equation}\tag{*}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:unnumbered:5}
\Tr_K(u)=|G|\footnote{If $X$ is a set, $|X|$ denotes the cardinality of $X$.}\,\Tr_B(u)(e).
\end{equation}
\end{statement}
It is enough to apply \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:corollary:5.10.11]{5.10.11} with $A=K$, noting that $\Tr_{B/K}:B_\natural\to K$ is given by
\begin{equation}\tag{5.11.2}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.11.2}
\Tr_{B/K}(g)=
\begin{cases}
|G|,& g=e,\\
0,& g\ne e.
\end{cases}
\end{equation}

\phantomsection\label{sga5:IIIB:proposition:5.11.3}\begin{statement}{Proposition 5.11.3}
Under the hypotheses of \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:proposition:5.11.1]{5.11.1}, let $g\in G$, let $Z_g$ be the centralizer of $g$ in $G$, and put $A=K[Z_g]$. Let $u\in\Hom_B(E,M\lten_KE)$, and let $gu\in\Hom_A(E,M\lten_KE)$ be the composite of the image of $u$ in $\Hom_A(E,M\lten_KE)$ with left multiplication by $g$. Then, in $H^0(T,M)$,
\[
\Tr_A(gu)(e)=\Tr_B(u)(g^{-1}).
\]
\end{statement}

We shall show more generally that there is a commutative square
\begin{equation}\tag{5.11.4}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.11.4}
\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=3.0em,row sep=2.2em]
\RHom_B(E,M\lten_KE) \arrow[r,"\Tr_B"] \arrow[d] &
M\lten_KB_\natural \arrow[r,"{x\mapsto x(g^{-1})}"] &
M \arrow[d,equal] \\
\RHom_A(E,M\lten_KE) \arrow[r,"\Tr_A"'] &
M\lten_KA_\natural \arrow[r,"{x\mapsto x(e)}"'] &
M,
\end{tikzcd}
\end{equation}
where the left vertical arrow is the composite of restriction of scalars and left multiplication by $g$. This square is the boundary of the diagram
\[
\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=2.8em,row sep=2.0em]
\RHom_B(E,M\lten_KE) \arrow[r,"\Tr_B"] \arrow[d,"\mathrm{rest}"'] &
M\lten_KB_\natural \arrow[r,"{x\mapsto x(g^{-1})}"] \arrow[d,"{\underset{(1)}{\Tr_{B/A}}}" description] &
M \arrow[d,equal] \\
\RHom_A(E,M\lten_KE) \arrow[r,"\Tr_A"'] \arrow[d,"{\RHom_A(E,s_g)}"'] &
M\lten_KA_\natural \arrow[d,"{\underset{(2)}{s_g}}" description] \arrow[dr,phantom,"\scriptstyle(3)" description] &
M \\
\RHom_A(E,M\lten_KE) \arrow[r,"\Tr_A"'] &
M\lten_KA_\natural \arrow[r,"{x\mapsto x(e)}"'] &
M,
\end{tikzcd}
\]
where $\mathrm{rest}$ denotes restriction of scalars and $s_g$ left multiplication by $g$. Square (1) commutes by \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:corollary:5.10.11]{5.10.11}, and (2) by the definition of $\Tr_A$. It therefore remains to prove the commutativity of (3), for which it is enough to show that, for $h\in G$,
\[
\Tr_A(x\mapsto gxh)(e)=\text{the value at }g^{-1}\text{ of the image of }h\text{ in }B,
\]
i.e.
\begin{equation}\tag{*}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:unnumbered:6}
\Tr_A(x\mapsto gxh)(e)=
\begin{cases}
1,& g^{-1}\text{ is conjugate to }h,\\
0,& \text{otherwise}.
\end{cases}
\end{equation}
For this one may replace $T$ by the punctual topos and $K$ by $\mathbb Z$, and it is then enough to prove (*) after multiplication by $|Z_g|$. In view of \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:proposition:5.11.1]{5.11.1}, we are therefore reduced to showing that
\[
\Tr_{\mathbb Z}(x\mapsto gxh)=
\begin{cases}
|Z_g|,& g^{-1}\text{ is conjugate to }h,\\
0,& \text{otherwise}.
\end{cases}
\]
But this follows from the fact that the left hand side is the number of fixed points of the map $x\mapsto gxh$ from $G$ to $G$, whence the commutativity of \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.11.4]{5.11.4} and the conclusion of \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:proposition:5.11.3]{5.11.3}.

\phantomsection\label{sga5:IIIB:corollary:5.11.5}\begin{statement}{Corollary 5.11.5}
Under the hypotheses of \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:proposition:5.11.3]{5.11.3}, one has
\begin{equation}\tag{5.11.5}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.11.5}
\Tr_K(gu)=|Z_g|\,\Tr_B(u)(g^{-1})=|Z_g|\,\Tr_A(gu)(e).
\end{equation}
\end{statement}

\subsubsection*{5.12. Traces and external tensor products}\phantomsection\label{sga5:IIIB:subsubsection:5.12}

Let, for $i=1,2$, $A_i$ be a flat $K$-algebra, and put $A=A_1\otimes_KA_2$. Let $E_i\in\ob D^-(A_i)$, $F_i\in\ob D^b(A_i^e)$ be such that $\RHom_{A_i}(E_i,F_i)\in\ob D^b(A_i^\circ)$, let $F=F_1\lten_KF_2\in\ob D^b(A^e)$, and suppose $\RHom_A(E,F)\in\ob D^b(A^\circ)$, where $E=E_1\lten_KE_2$. Then there is a commutative square
\begin{equation}\tag{5.12.1}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.12.1}
\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=large,row sep=large]
(\RHom_{A_1}(E_1,F_1)\lten_{A_1}E_1)\lten_K(\RHom_{A_2}(E_2,F_2)\lten_{A_2}E_2) \arrow[r,"(1)"] \arrow[d,"(2)"'] & (F_1\lten_{A_1^e}A_1)\lten_K(F_2\lten_{A_2^e}A_2) \arrow[d,"(3)"]\\
\RHom_A(E,F)\lten_AE \arrow[r] & F\lten_{A^e}A,
\end{tikzcd}
\end{equation}
where (1) is the tensor product of the arrows \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.3.5]{5.3.5}, (2) is defined by the tensor-product arrow
\begin{equation}\tag{5.12.2}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.12.2}
\RHom_{A_1}(E_1,F_1)\lten_K\RHom_{A_2}(E_2,F_2)\longrightarrow\RHom_A(E,F),
\end{equation}
and (3) is the evident canonical isomorphism. The verification is essentially trivial. One checks in the same way that, for $E_i\in\ob D^-(A_i)$, $F_i\in\ob D^+(A_i^e)$, $G_i\in\ob D^-(A_i)$, with $F_i\lten_{A_i}G_i\in\ob D^b(A_i)$, $\RHom_{A_i}(E_i,F_i)\in\ob D^b(A_i^\circ)$, $\RHom_{A_i}(E_i,F_i\lten_{A_i}G_i)\in\ob D^b(K)$, $F\lten_AG\in\ob D^b(A)$, where $G=G_1\lten_KG_2$, there is a commutative square
\begin{equation}\tag{5.12.3}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.12.3}
{\scriptsize
\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=2.8em,row sep=large]
\begin{gathered}
(\RHom_{A_1}(E_1,F_1)\lten_{A_1}G_1)\lten_K{}\\[-.15em]
(\RHom_{A_2}(E_2,F_2)\lten_{A_2}G_2)
\end{gathered}
\arrow[r,"(1)"] \arrow[d,"(2)"']
&
\begin{gathered}
\RHom_{A_1}(E_1,F_1\lten_{A_1}G_1)\lten_K{}\\[-.15em]
\RHom_{A_2}(E_2,F_2\lten_{A_2}G_2)
\end{gathered}
\arrow[d,"(3)"]\\
\RHom_A(E,F)\lten_AG \arrow[r,"{\hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.4.2]{5.4.2}}"'] & \RHom_A(E,F\lten_AG),
\end{tikzcd}
}
\end{equation}
where (2) and (3) are tensor-product arrows of the type \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.12.2]{5.12.2}, and (1) is the tensor product of the arrows \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.4.2]{5.4.2}. From the commutativity of the squares \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.12.1]{5.12.1} and \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.12.3]{5.12.3} it follows that, for $E_i\in\ob D(A_i)_{\mathrm{parf}}$, $F_i\in\ob D^b(A_i^e)$ such that $F=F_1\lten_KF_2\in\ob D^b(A^e)$, there is a commutative square
\begin{equation}\tag{5.12.4}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.12.4}
\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=large,row sep=large]
\RHom_{A_1}(E_1,F_1\lten_{A_1}E_1)\lten_K\RHom_{A_2}(E_2,F_2\lten_{A_2}E_2) \arrow[r,"(1)"] \arrow[d,"(2)"'] & (F_1\lten_{A_1^e}A_1)\lten_K(F_2\lten_{A_2^e}A_2) \arrow[d,"(3)"]\\
\RHom_A(E,F\lten_AE) \arrow[r,"{\hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.6.1]{5.6.1}}"'] & F\lten_{A^e}A,
\end{tikzcd}
\end{equation}
where (1) is the tensor product of the arrows $\Tr_{A_i}$ \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.6.1]{(5.6.1)}, (2) is defined by \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.12.2]{5.12.2}, and (3) is the canonical isomorphism appearing in \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.12.1]{5.12.1}. Consequently, for $u_i\in\Hom_{A_i}(E_i,F_i\lten_{A_i}E_i)$, one has
\begin{equation}\tag{5.12.5}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.12.5}
\Tr_A(u_1\lten_Ku_2)=\Tr_{A_1}(u_1)\Tr_{A_2}(u_2),
\end{equation}
the product on the right being defined by the canonical arrow
\[
H^0(T,F_1\lten_{A_1^e}A_1)\otimes H^0(T,F_2\lten_{A_2^e}A_2)
\longrightarrow H^0(T,F\lten_{A^e}A).
\]

\subsubsection*{5.13. Traces of semilinear endomorphisms}\phantomsection\label{sga5:IIIB:subsubsection:5.13}

Let $A$ be a $K$-algebra and $\varphi$ an endomorphism of $A$. If $E$, $F$ are left $A$-modules, recall that an additive homomorphism $u:E\to F$ is called $\varphi$-$A$-linear if $u(ax)=\varphi(a)u(x)$ for all $a\in A$, $x\in E$, i.e. if $u$ is an $A$-linear homomorphism from $E$ to the $A$-module ${}_{(\varphi)}F$ deduced from $F$ by restriction of scalars along $\varphi$. If $F$ is an $A$-bimodule and $G$ a left $A$-module, there is an evident isomorphism
\[
{}_{(\varphi)}F\otimes_AG\xrightarrow{\sim}{}_{(\varphi)}(F\otimes_AG),
\]
which derives trivially to
\[
{}_{(\varphi)}F\lten_AG\xrightarrow{\sim}{}_{(\varphi)}(F\lten_AG).
\]
Assuming $A$ flat over $K$, one deduces from \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.4.2]{5.4.2} the arrow
\begin{equation}\tag{5.13.1}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.13.1}
\RHom_A(E,{}_{(\varphi)}F)\lten_AG\longrightarrow\RHom_A(E,{}_{(\varphi)}(F\lten_AG))
\end{equation}
for $E\in\ob D^-(A)$, $F\in\ob D^+(A^e)$, $G\in\ob D^-(A)$, with $F\lten_AG\in\ob D^+(A)$. For $E\in\ob D(A)_{\mathrm{parf}}$, \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.13.1]{5.13.1} is an isomorphism, which allows one to define, by \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.3.5]{5.3.5}, a trace arrow
\begin{equation}\tag{5.13.2}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.13.2}
\Tr_{A,\varphi}:\RHom_A(E,{}_{(\varphi)}(F\lten_AE))\longrightarrow {}_{(\varphi)}F\lten_{A^e}A.
\end{equation}
In particular, for $F=A$, one obtains
\begin{equation}\tag{5.13.3}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.13.3}
\Tr_{A,\varphi}:\Hom_A(E,{}_{(\varphi)}E)\longrightarrow H^0(T,A_{\natural,\varphi}),
\end{equation}
where $A_{\natural,\varphi}={}_{(\varphi)}A\otimes_{A^e}A$ is the quotient $K$-module of $A$ by the submodule locally generated by local sections of the form $ab-\varphi(b)a$. In the case where $A=K[G]$, $G$ is a discrete group, and $\varphi$ is defined by an endomorphism $\varphi$ of $G$, $A_{\natural,\varphi}$ is the free $K$-module with basis $G_{\natural,\varphi}$, where $G_{\natural,\varphi}$ is the quotient of $G$ by the action $(g,x)\mapsto\varphi(g)xg^{-1}$; the orbits are sometimes called $\varphi$-conjugacy classes of $G$. The traces \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.13.2]{5.13.2} coincide, for $T$ punctual, with those considered by Grothendieck and discussed in \hyperref[sga5:XII:expose:xii]{Exposé XII}.

In the situation of \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:subsubsection:5.10]{5.10}, if $\varphi$, $\psi$ are respective endomorphisms of $A$, $B$, compatible with $A\to B$, there is a unique arrow
\[
\Tr_A:B_{\natural,\psi}\longrightarrow A_{\natural,\varphi}
\]
making the square, analogous to \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.10.2]{5.10.2},
\begin{equation}\tag{5.13.4}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.13.4}
\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=large,row sep=large]
{}_{(\psi)}B=\Hom_B(B,{}_{(\psi)}B) \arrow[r,"\Tr_{B,\psi}"] \arrow[d] & B_{\natural,\psi} \arrow[d,"\Tr_A"]\\
\Hom_A(B,{}_{(\varphi)}A)\otimes_AB \arrow[r,"\mathrm{ev}"'] & A_{\natural,\varphi},
\end{tikzcd}
\end{equation}
commute, where $\mathrm{ev}$ is the evaluation arrow, and the left vertical arrow is restriction of scalars. One defines an arrow analogous to \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.10.5]{5.10.5}, with $A\otimes_KM$ (resp. $B\otimes_KM$) replaced by ${}_{(\varphi)}A\otimes_KM$ (resp. ${}_{(\psi)}B\otimes_KM$), and there are compatibilities analogous to \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:proposition:5.10.6]{5.10.6}, \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:proposition:5.10.9]{5.10.9}, \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:corollary:5.10.11]{5.10.11}, which we leave to the reader to state.

Let $G$ be a finite group and $\varphi$ an endomorphism of $G$. For $g\in G$, let $Z_{g,\varphi}$ be the $\varphi$-centralizer of $g$ in $G$, i.e. the set of $x\in G$ such that $\varphi(x)gx^{-1}=g$. Put $A=K[G]$, and let $E\in\ob D(A)_{\mathrm{parf}}$, $M\in\ob D^b(K)$. The preceding compatibilities easily imply that, for $u\in\Hom_A(E,{}_{(\varphi)}(M\lten_KE))$ and $g\in G$,
\begin{equation}\tag{5.13.5}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:5.13.5}
\Tr_K(gu)=|Z_{g^{-1},\varphi}|\,\Tr_{A,\varphi}(u)(g^{-1}),
\end{equation}
the details of the verification being left to the reader.

\section*{6. Equivariant Correspondences and Divisibility of Local Terms}\phantomsection\label{sga5:IIIB:section:6}

\subsection*{6.1. Notation}\phantomsection\label{sga5:IIIB:subsection:6.1}

In this number, $S$ denotes the spectrum of a field of characteristic $p$, and $\Lambda$ a noetherian commutative ring annihilated by an integer not divisible by $p$. The $S$-schemes considered will be assumed separated and of finite type. The $\Lambda$-algebras considered will be associative, unital, and of finite type, but not necessarily commutative. Most often, and in particular for everything concerning “non-commutative Lefschetz--Verdier local terms,” they will be assumed flat; in applications, they will be algebras of finite groups.

If $X$ is an $S$-scheme and $A$ a $\Lambda$-algebra, we write $D({}_AX)$ (resp. $D(X_A)$) for the derived category of the category of sheaves of left (resp. right) $A$-modules on $X$. For $A$ commutative, we shall also write $D(X,A)$ for $D({}_AX)$, and $D(X)=D(X,\Lambda)$. We denote by $D_c(-)$ (resp. $D_{\mathrm{ctf}}(-)$) the full subcategory formed by complexes with constructible cohomology (resp. finite tor-dimension and constructible cohomology). If $A$, $B$ are $\Lambda$-algebras, we write $D({}_AX_B)$ for the derived category of sheaves of $(A,B)$-bimodules, left over $A$ and right over $B$. Finally, we use the notations $A^o$, $A^e$, $A_{\natural}$ of \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:subsubsection:5.1]{5.1}.

\subsection*{6.2}\phantomsection\label{sga5:IIIB:subsection:6.2}
The formalism of (III 1, 2, 3) extends without difficulty to the “non-commutative” case. We briefly indicate the main points of this generalization.

Let $X$ be an $S$-scheme and let $A$, $B$ be $\Lambda$-algebras. If $L\in\Ob D_{\mathrm{ctf}}(X_A)$ and $M\in\Ob D({}_AX_B)$, with $M$ of finite tor-dimension and constructible cohomology as an object of $D(X_B)$, then
\[
L\lten_A M\in\Ob D(X_B)_{\mathrm{ctf}}
\]
as is immediate. On the other hand, it follows from (SGA $4^{1/2}$ Finiteness 1.6) that, for $M$ as above and $L\in\Ob D_{\mathrm{ctf}}({}_AX)$, one has
\[
\RHom_A(L,M)\in\Ob D_{\mathrm{ctf}}(X_B).
\]
It also follows from loc. cit. that, if $f$ is an $S$-morphism, $D_{\mathrm{ctf}}({}_A-)$ is stable under the operations $(f^*,f_*,f_!,f^!)$, the case of $f^*$ being trivial.

Let, for $i=1,2$, $f_i:X_i\to Y_i$ be an $S$-morphism,
\[
f=f_1\times_S f_2:X=X_1\times_SX_2\longrightarrow Y=Y_1\times_SY_2,
\]
and let $A$ be a $\Lambda$-algebra. For $L_1\in\Ob D^-(X_{1A})$, $L_2\in\Ob D^-({}_AX_2)$, there is a Künneth arrow in $D^-(Y)$
\begin{equation}
f_{1!}L_1\lten_{S,A}f_{2!}L_2
\longrightarrow
f_*(L_1\lten_{S,A}L_2),
\tag{6.2.1}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:6.2.1}
\end{equation}
where
\[
L_1\lten_{S,A}L_2
\stackrel{\mathrm{dfn}}{=}p_1^*L_1\lten_Ap_2^*L_2.
\]
As in (\hyperref[sga5:III:subsection:1.6]{III 1.6}), one deduces that it is an isomorphism. If $B$, $C$ are $\Lambda$-algebras, with $B$ or $C$ assumed flat over $\Lambda$ in order to define
\[
\lten_A:D^-({}_B{-}_A)\times D^-({}_A{-}_C)\longrightarrow D^-({}_B{-}_C),
\]
there is an isomorphism analogous to \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:6.2.1]{6.2.1} in $D^-({}_BY_C)$, for $L_1\in\Ob D^-({}_BX_{1A})$, $L_2\in\Ob D^-({}_AX_{2B})$. One checks similarly that, for $M_1\in\Ob D^-(Y_{1A})$, $M_2\in\Ob D^-({}_AY_2)$, there is a Künneth isomorphism in $D^-(X)$
\begin{equation}
f^!M_1\lten_{S,A}f^!M_2
\longrightarrow
f^!(M_1\lten_{S,A}M_2),
\tag{6.2.2}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:6.2.2}
\end{equation}
and an analogous isomorphism in $D^-({}_B X_C)$ for $M_1\in\Ob D^-({}_BY_{1A})$, $M_2\in\Ob D^-({}_AY_{2C})$.

Finally, let, for $i=1,2$, $A_i$ be a $\Lambda$-algebra, $A_2$ being flat, $E_1\in\Ob D_{\mathrm{ctf}}({}_{A_1}X_1)$, $F_2\in\Ob D^+({}_{A_2}X_2)$, $F_1\in\Ob D({}_{A_1}X_{1A_2})$, with $F_1$ of finite tor-dimension and constructible cohomology as an object of $D(X_{1A_2})$. Then
\[
\RHom_{A_1}(E_1,F_1)\in\Ob D_{\mathrm{ctf}}(X_{1A_2}),
\]
as seen above, and there is a canonical arrow in $D^+(X)$ ($D^+(X,A_2)$ when $A_2$ is commutative)
\begin{equation}
\RHom_{A_1}(E_1,F_1)\lten_{S,A_2}F_2
\longrightarrow
\RHom_{A_1}(p_1^*E_1,F_1\lten_{S,A_2}F_2),
\tag{6.2.3}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:6.2.3}
\end{equation}
defined by composing the “inverse-image” arrow (\hyperref[sga5:III:equation:2.1.1]{III 2.1.1})
\[
p_1^*\RHom_{A_1}(E_1,F_1)\lten_{A_2}p_2^*F_2
\longrightarrow
\RHom_{A_1}(p_1^*E_1,p_1^*F_1)\lten_{A_2}p_2^*F_2
\]
with the arrow analogous to \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.4.2]{5.4.2}
\[
\RHom_{A_1}(p_1^*E_1,p_1^*F_1)\lten_{A_2}p_2^*F_2
\longrightarrow
\RHom_{A_1}(p_1^*E_1,p_1^*F_1\lten_{A_2}p_2^*F_2),
\]
adjoint, by \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.2.3]{5.2.3}, to the arrow
\[
p_1^*E_1\lten\RHom_{A_1}(p_1^*E_1,p_1^*F_1)\lten_{A_2}p_2^*F_2
\longrightarrow
p_1^*F_1\lten_{A_2}p_2^*F_2
\]
defined by the evaluation arrow \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.3.6]{5.3.6}. One deduces from \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:6.2.1]{6.2.1}, as in (\hyperref[sga5:III:proposition:2.3]{III 2.3}), that \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:6.2.3]{6.2.3} is an isomorphism. In particular, for $A_2=\Lambda$, $F_2=\Lambda_{X_2}$, \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:6.2.3]{6.2.3} gives an isomorphism
\begin{equation}
p_1^*\RHom_{A_1}(E_1,F_1)
\xrightarrow{\sim}
\RHom_{A_1}(p_1^*E_1,p_1^*F_1).
\tag{6.2.4}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:6.2.4}
\end{equation}

\subsection*{6.3}\phantomsection\label{sga5:IIIB:subsection:6.3}
If $t:T\to S$ is an $S$-scheme, and $A$ is a $\Lambda$-algebra, we put
\begin{equation}
t_A^! = KA_T \qquad(\in\Ob D^+({}_AT_A)).
\tag{6.3.1}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:6.3.1}
\end{equation}
For $A=\Lambda$, we write $K_T$ instead of $K\Lambda_T$. It follows from \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:6.2.2]{6.2.2} that there is a canonical isomorphism in $D^+({}_AT_A)$
\begin{equation}
K_T\lten A\xrightarrow{\sim}KA_T.
\tag{6.3.2}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:6.3.2}
\end{equation}
We denote by $D_A$ the functor
\[
\RHom_A(-,KA_T).
\]
It sends $D_{\mathrm{ctf}}({}_AT)$ into $D_{\mathrm{ctf}}(T_A)$ and conversely, and the proof of (SGA $4^{1/2}$ Finiteness Theorem 4) shows that, for $L\in\Ob D_{\mathrm{ctf}}({}_AT)$, the canonical arrow
\[
L\longrightarrow D_AD_AL
\]
is an isomorphism. For $A=\Lambda$, we write $D$ instead of $D_A$.

\subsection*{6.4}\phantomsection\label{sga5:IIIB:subsection:6.4}
Let $A$ be a flat $\Lambda$-algebra, and let, for $i=1,2$, $X_i$ be an $S$-scheme, $X=X_1\times_SX_2$, $p_i:X\to X_i$ the projections, and $L_i\in\Ob D_{\mathrm{ctf}}({}_AX_i)$. By \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:6.2.3]{6.2.3} applied to $A_1=A_2=A$, $E_1=L_1$, $F_2=L_2$, $E_2=KA_{X_1}$, there is an isomorphism
\[
D_A L_1\lten_{S,A}L_2
\xrightarrow{\sim}
\RHom_A(p_1^*L_1,KA_{X_1}\lten_{S,A}L_2),
\]
whence, by composing with the Künneth isomorphism \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:6.2.2]{6.2.2}
\[
KA_{X_1}\lten_{S,A}L_2\xrightarrow{\sim}p_2^!L_2,
\]
an isomorphism
\begin{equation}
D_AL_1\lten_{S,A}L_2
\xrightarrow{\sim}
\RHom_A(p_1^*L_1,p_2^!L_2),
\tag{6.4.1}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:6.4.1}
\end{equation}
which generalizes (\hyperref[sga5:III:equation:3.1.1]{III 3.1.1}).

Let $c:C\to X$ be an $S$-morphism, and put $c_i=p_ic$. By the induction formula (SGA 4 XVIII 3.1.12.2), there is a canonical isomorphism, analogous to (\hyperref[sga5:III:equation:3.2.1]{III 3.2.1}),
\begin{equation}
c^!\RHom_A(p_1^*L_1,p_2^!L_2)
\xrightarrow{\sim}
\RHom_A(c_1^*L_1,c_2^!L_2),
\tag{6.4.2}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:6.4.2}
\end{equation}
and hence, by composing with \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:6.4.1]{6.4.1}, an isomorphism
\begin{equation}
H^0(X,c_*c^!(D_AL_1\lten_{S,A}L_2))
\xrightarrow{\sim}
\Hom_A(c_1^*L_1,c_2^!L_2).
\tag{6.4.3}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:6.4.3}
\end{equation}
The elements of $\Hom_A(c_1^*L_1,c_2^!L_2)$ will be called cohomological correspondences from $L_1$ to $L_2$ with support in $c$. The terminology differs slightly from that of loc. cit.; the two terminologies coincide when $c$ is proper, which is the case in practice.

Given $S$-morphisms $f_i:X_i\to Y_i$ $(i=1,2)$ and a commutative square (\hyperref[sga5:III:equation:3.7.1]{III 3.7.1}) with $c$ proper, one defines, as in loc. cit., direct-image arrows analogous to (\hyperref[sga5:III:equation:3.7.5]{III 3.7.5}), (\hyperref[sga5:III:equation:3.7.6]{III 3.7.6}), for $L_i\in\Ob D_{\mathrm{ctf}}({}_AX_i)$.

\subsection*{6.5}\phantomsection\label{sga5:IIIB:subsection:6.5}
Let $A$ be a flat $\Lambda$-algebra, $X$ an $S$-scheme, $X^2=X\times_SX$, $p_1,p_2:X^2\to X$ the canonical projections, $\delta:X\to X^2$ the diagonal, and
\[
c:C\longrightarrow X^2
\]
an $S$-morphism. Put $c_i=p_ic$, and let $X^c=C\times_{X^2}X$ be the scheme of fixed points of $c$, giving a cartesian square
\begin{equation}
\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=large,row sep=large]
X^c \arrow[r,"i^c"] \arrow[d,"\delta^c"'] & X \arrow[d,"\delta"]\\
C \arrow[r,"c"'] & X^2 .
\end{tikzcd}
\tag{6.5.1}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:6.5.1}
\end{equation}
Put
\begin{equation}
KA_{X,L_{\natural}}=K_X\lten_{\Lambda}(A\lten_{A^e}A),
\qquad
KA_{X,\natural}=K_X\lten_{\Lambda}A_{\natural}.
\tag{6.5.2}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:6.5.2}
\end{equation}
Let $L\in\Ob D_{\mathrm{ctf}}({}_AX)$. We define a “local trace” homomorphism
\begin{equation}
\Tr_A:\delta^*\RHom_A(p_1^*L,p_2^!L)
\longrightarrow
KA_{X,L_{\natural}},
\tag{6.5.3}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:6.5.3}
\end{equation}
by composing the isomorphism
\begin{equation}
\delta^*\RHom_A(p_1^*L,p_2^!L)
\xrightarrow{\sim}
\delta^*(D_AL\lten_{S,A}L)
\tag{6.5.4}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:6.5.4}
\end{equation}
deduced from the inverse of \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:6.4.1]{6.4.1} by applying $\delta^*$, the trivial isomorphism
\begin{equation}
\delta^*(D_AL\lten_{S,A}L)=D_AL\lten_AL,
\tag{6.5.5}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:6.5.5}
\end{equation}
and the evaluation arrow \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.3.5]{5.3.5}
\begin{equation}
D_AL\lten_AL\longrightarrow KA_{X,L_{\natural}}.
\tag{6.5.6}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:6.5.6}
\end{equation}
(Here $KA_X\lten_{A^e}A$ is identified with $KA_{X,L_{\natural}}$ by means of \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:6.3.2]{6.3.2}, $K_X\otimes A=KA_X$.)
It follows at once from the definition that, for $X=S$, \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:6.5.3]{6.5.3} coincides with the arrow
\[
\Tr_A:\RHom_A(L,L)\longrightarrow A\lten_{A^e}A
\]
of \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.6.1]{5.6.1}. Composing the arrow deduced from \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:6.5.3]{6.5.3} by applying $i^cc^!$ with the arrow
\begin{equation}
\delta^*c_*^c\RHom_A(c_1^*L,c_2^!L)
\longrightarrow
i_*^ci^{c!}\delta^*\RHom_A(p_1^*L,p_2^!L)
\tag{6.5.7}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:6.5.7}
\end{equation}
deduced from \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:6.4.2]{6.4.2} and the base-change arrow
\[
\delta^*c_*c^!\longrightarrow i_*^ci^{c!}\delta^*,
\]
one obtains an arrow
\begin{equation}
\Tr_A:\delta^*c_*\RHom_A(c_1^*L,c_2^!L)
\longrightarrow
i_*^ci^{c!}KA_{X,L_{\natural}}
=i_*^cKA_{X^c,L_{\natural}},
\tag{6.5.8}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:6.5.8}
\end{equation}
and hence the arrow
\begin{equation}
\Tr_A:\Hom_A(c_1^*L,c_2^!L)
\longrightarrow
H^0(X^c,KA_{X^c,L_{\natural}}).
\tag{6.5.9}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:6.5.9}
\end{equation}
We again write $\Tr_A$ for the composites of \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:6.5.3]{6.5.3}, \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:6.5.8]{6.5.8}, \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:6.5.9]{6.5.9} with the arrows deduced from the canonical arrow
\[
KA_{X,L_{\natural}}\longrightarrow KA_{X,\natural}.
\]
One checks easily that, for $A=\Lambda$, one has, for $u\in\Hom(c_1^*L,c_2^!L)$,
\begin{equation}
\Tr(u)=\langle u,1\rangle,
\tag{6.5.10}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:6.5.10}
\end{equation}
with the notation of (\hyperref[sga5:III:equation:4.2.5]{III 4.2.5}), where $1$ denotes the diagonal correspondence. More precisely, the arrow
\[
c_*\RHom(c_1^*L,c_2^!L)\longrightarrow \delta_*i_*^cK_{X^c}
\]
adjoint to \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:6.5.8]{6.5.8} is the one defined by (\hyperref[sga5:III:equation:4.2.4]{III 4.2.4}') with $X_1=X_2$, $L_1=L_2$, $d=\delta$, and the diagonal correspondence $1\in\Hom(L,L)$.

\subsection*{6.6}\phantomsection\label{sga5:IIIB:subsection:6.6}
Let $A$ be a flat $\Lambda$-algebra, and let, for $i=1,2$, $X_i$ be an $S$-scheme, $X=X_1\times_SX_2$, and $L_i\in\Ob D_{\mathrm{ctf}}({}_AX_i)$. We define a pairing
\begin{equation}
\RHom_A(p_1^*L_1,p_2^!L_2)
\lten_{\Lambda}
\RHom_A(p_2^*L_2,p_1^!L_1)
\longrightarrow
KA_{X,L_{\natural}}
\tag{6.6.1}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:6.6.1}
\end{equation}
as follows. One identifies the left hand side with
\[
\RHom_A(p_1^*L_1,p_1^*KA_{X_1})
\lten_A p_2^*L_2
\lten_{\Lambda}
\RHom_A(p_2^*L_2,p_2^*KA_{X_2})
\lten_A p_1^*L_1
\]
by the isomorphisms \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:6.4.1]{6.4.1}, then with
\[
A\lten_{A^e}\RHom_A(\ )\lten_{\Lambda}p_2^*L_2
 \lten_{\Lambda}\RHom_A(\ )\lten_{\Lambda}p_1^*L_1\lten_{A^e}A
\]
by \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.3.1]{5.3.1}. This object is sent to
\[
A\lten_{A^e}(KA_{X_1}\lten_{\Lambda}KA_{X_2})\lten_{A^e}A=R
\]
by the tensor product of the evaluation arrows \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.3.6]{5.3.6}. Finally $R$ is identified with
\[
A\lten_{A^e}(KA_{X_1}\lten_AKA_{X_2})
\]
by \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.8.2]{5.8.2}, and then with $KA_{X,L_{\natural}}$ by \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:6.5.2]{6.5.2} and (\hyperref[sga5:III:equation:1.7.6]{III 1.7.6}).

If $c:C\to X$, $d:D\to X$ are correspondences, and
\[
e=c\times_Xd:E\to X,
\]
one deduces from \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:6.6.1]{6.6.1}, as in (\hyperref[sga5:III:subsection:4.2]{III 4.2}), a pairing
\begin{equation}
c_*\RHom_A(c_1^*L_1,c_2^!L_2)
\lten_{\Lambda}
d_*\RHom_A(d_2^*L_2,d_1^!L_1)
\longrightarrow
e_*KA_{E,L_{\natural}},
\tag{6.6.2}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:6.6.2}
\end{equation}
and hence a cup-product
\begin{equation}
\langle\ ,\ \rangle_A:
\Hom_A(c_1^*L_1,c_2^!L_2)\otimes
\Hom_A(d_2^*L_2,d_1^!L_1)
\longrightarrow
H^0(E,KA_{E,L_{\natural}}).
\tag{6.6.3}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:6.6.3}
\end{equation}
It is not difficult to extend to the non-commutative case the notion of composition of correspondences studied in (\hyperref[sga5:III:subsection:5.2]{III 5.2}), and one checks that, for
\[
u\in\Hom_A(c_1^*L_1,c_2^!L_2),\qquad
v\in\Hom_A(d_2^*L_2,d_1^!L_1),
\]
one has the formula analogous to (\hyperref[sga5:III:equation:5.2.9]{III 5.2.9})
\begin{equation}
\langle u,v\rangle_A=\Tr_A(\bar vu)=\Tr_A(u\bar v).
\tag{6.6.4}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:6.6.4}
\end{equation}
The Lefschetz formula (\hyperref[sga5:III:theorem:4.4]{III 4.4}) generalizes without difficulty in this context. Given proper $S$-morphisms $f_i:X_i\to Y_i$ $(i=1,2)$ and a commutative cube (\hyperref[sga5:III:equation:4.4.0]{III 4.4.0}), for $L_i\in\Ob D_{\mathrm{ctf}}({}_AX_i)$ one has a commutative square analogous to (\hyperref[sga5:III:equation:4.4.1]{III 4.4.1}), with $K_C$ (resp. $K_D$) replaced by $KA_{C,L_{\natural}}$ (resp. $KA_{D,L_{\natural}}$), and a formula analogous to (\hyperref[sga5:III:equation:4.5.1]{III 4.5.1}), in $H^0(D,KA_{D,L_{\natural}})$:
\begin{equation}
\langle f_*u,f_*v\rangle_A=g_*\langle u,v\rangle_A.
\tag{6.6.5}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:6.6.5}
\end{equation}
The arrow
\[
f_*c_*KA_{C,L_{\natural}}\longrightarrow d_*KA_{D,L_{\natural}}
\]
is deduced from arrow (4) of (\hyperref[sga5:III:equation:4.4.1]{III 4.4.1}) by tensoring with
\[
A\lten_{A^e}A .
\]

\subsection*{6.7}\phantomsection\label{sga5:IIIB:subsection:6.7}
Let $X$ and $c$ be as in \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:subsection:6.5]{6.5}, and let $A\to B$ be a morphism of flat $\Lambda$-algebras. With respect to extension and restriction of scalars, the arrows $\Tr_A$ (\hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:6.5.3]{6.5.3}, \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:6.5.8]{6.5.8}, \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:6.5.9]{6.5.9}) behave like the arrows $\Tr_A$ of \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.6.1]{5.6.1}, \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.6.2]{5.6.2}. The point is that, if $f$ is an $S$-morphism, the functors $(f^*,f_*,f_!,f^!)$ commute with extension and restriction of scalars; consequently, the study of the behavior of $\Tr_A$ \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:6.5.3]{(6.5.3)} with respect to extension and restriction of scalars reduces to the behavior of the “tensor-product” arrow
\[
\RHom_A(p_1^*L,p_1^*KA_X)\lten_Ap_2^*L
\longrightarrow
\RHom_A(p_1^*L,p_1^*KA_X\lten_Ap_2^*L)
\]
and of the “evaluation” arrow
\[
\RHom_A(L,KA_X)\lten_A L\longrightarrow KA_{X,L_{\natural}}.
\]
Thus the commutativity of the squares \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.9.1]{5.9.1}, \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.9.2]{5.9.2} implies the commutativity of the square
\begin{equation}
\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=huge,row sep=large]
\delta^*c_*\RHom_A(c_1^*L,c_2^!L) \arrow[r,"\Tr_A"] \arrow[d]
& i_*^cKA_{X^c,L_{\natural}} \arrow[d]\\
\delta^*c_*\RHom_B(c_1^*(B\lten_A L),c_2^!(B\lten_A L)) \arrow[r,"\Tr_B"']
& i_*^cKB_{X^c,L_{\natural}},
\end{tikzcd}
\tag{6.7.1}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:6.7.1}
\end{equation}
where the left vertical arrow is extension of scalars and the right vertical arrow is defined by the canonical arrow
\[
A\lten_{A^e}A\longrightarrow B\lten_{B^e}B.
\]
It follows that, for $u\in\Hom_A(c_1^*L,c_2^!L)$,
\begin{equation}
\Tr_B(B\lten_Au)=\varphi\Tr_A(u),
\tag{6.7.2}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:6.7.2}
\end{equation}
where
\[
\varphi:H^0(X^c,KA_{X^c,L_{\natural}})\longrightarrow H^0(X^c,KB_{X^c,L_{\natural}})
\]
is the canonical map.

Assume $A$ is finite type and flat as a $\Lambda$-module, $B$ finite type and flat as a left $A$-module, and $A_{\natural}$, $B_{\natural}$ flat over $\Lambda$. There is a map $\Tr_{B/A}:B_{\natural}\to A_{\natural}$; we shall again denote by
\begin{equation}
\Tr_{B/A}:KB_{X,\natural}\longrightarrow KA_{X,\natural}
\tag{6.7.3}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:6.7.3}
\end{equation}
the arrow deduced from it by applying $K_X\lten -$ \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:6.5.2]{(6.5.2)}. It follows from \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:proposition:5.10.6]{5.10.6} and \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:proposition:5.10.9]{5.10.9} that, for $L\in\Ob D_{\mathrm{ctf}}({}_BX)$, there is a commutative square
\begin{equation}
\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=huge,row sep=large]
\delta^*c_*\RHom_B(c_1^*L,c_2^!L) \arrow[r,"\Tr_B"] \arrow[d]
& i_*^cKB_{X^c,\natural} \arrow[d,"\Tr_{B/A}"]\\
\delta^*c_*\RHom_A(c_1^*L,c_2^!L) \arrow[r,"\Tr_A"']
& i_*^cKA_{X^c,\natural},
\end{tikzcd}
\tag{6.7.4}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:6.7.4}
\end{equation}
where the left vertical arrow is restriction of scalars. Consequently, for $u\in\Hom_B(c_1^*L,c_2^!L)$, one has, in $H^0(X^c,KA_{X^c,\natural})$,
\begin{equation}
\Tr_A(u)=\Tr_{B/A}(\Tr_B(u)).
\tag{6.7.5}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:6.7.5}
\end{equation}

\subsection*{6.8}\phantomsection\label{sga5:IIIB:subsection:6.8}
Let $A$, $B$ be flat $\Lambda$-algebras, $X$, $Y$ $S$-schemes, $Z=X\times_SY$, and let $c:C\to X^2$, $d:D\to Y^2$ be correspondences. Let
\[
e=c\times_Sd:E=C\times_SD\longrightarrow Z^2
\]
be their product. Then
\[
Z^e=X^c\times_SY^d.
\]
Put $R=A\otimes_{\Lambda}B$. As in (\hyperref[sga5:III:subsection:5.3]{III 5.3}), one defines the external tensor product
\begin{equation}
\Hom_A(c_1^*L,c_2^!L)\otimes \Hom_B(d_1^*M,d_2^!M)
\xrightarrow{\ \lten_S\ }
\Hom_R(e_1^*(L\lten_SM),e_2^!(L\lten_SM)),
\tag{6.8.1}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:6.8.1}
\end{equation}
and one deduces easily from the commutativity of the squares \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.12.1]{5.12.1}, \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.12.3]{5.12.3} that, for
\[
u\in\Hom_A(c_1^*L,c_2^!L),\qquad v\in\Hom_B(d_1^*M,d_2^!M),
\]
one has, in $H^0(Z^e,KR_{Z^e,L_{\natural}})$,
\begin{equation}
\Tr_R(u\lten_Sv)=\Tr_A(u)\Tr_B(v),
\tag{6.8.2}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:6.8.2}
\end{equation}
the product on the right being defined by the canonical arrow
\begin{equation}
H^0(X^c,KA_{X^c,L_{\natural}})\otimes
H^0(Y^d,KB_{Y^d,L_{\natural}})
\longrightarrow
H^0(Z^e,KR_{Z^e,L_{\natural}}),
\tag{6.8.3}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:6.8.3}
\end{equation}
coming from the isomorphism
\[
KA_{X^c,L_{\natural}}\lten_S KB_{Y^d,L_{\natural}}\longrightarrow K(A\otimes B)_{Z^e,L_{\natural}}
\]
given by (\hyperref[sga5:III:equation:1.7.6]{III 1.7.6}) and arrow (4) of \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.9.1]{5.9.1}.

\subsection*{6.9}\phantomsection\label{sga5:IIIB:subsection:6.9}
Let $G$ be a finite group. If $X$ is a $G$-$S$-scheme, that is, an $S$-scheme endowed with an action of $G$ by $S$-automorphisms,\footnote{We agree that $G$ acts on spaces on the right.} and $A$ is a $\Lambda$-algebra,\footnote{We are chiefly interested in the case of commutative $\Lambda$-algebras.} we denote by $D(G,{}_AX)$ the derived category of the category of sheaves of left $G$-$A$-modules on $X$. For the notions of $G$-sheaf of sets, rings, modules, and so on, on $X$ for the étale topology, we refer the reader to (X 1). We denote by the subscript $c$ (resp. ctf) the full subcategory formed by complexes of $G$-$A$-modules which, as complexes of $A$-modules, have constructible cohomology (resp. finite tor-dimension and constructible cohomology).

Similarly, if $B$ is a $\Lambda$-algebra, we denote by $D(G,{}_AX_B)$ the derived category of sheaves of $G$-$(A,B)$-bimodules on $X$. If $G$ acts trivially on $X$, a left $G$-$A$-module on $X$ is just a left $A[G]$-module on $X$ without operators, hence
\[
D(G,{}_AX)=D({}_{A[G]}X).
\]
Since $G$ is finite, one also has
\[
D_c({}_AX)=D_c({}_{A[G]}X),
\]
but only an inclusion
\[
D_{\mathrm{ctf}}({}_{A[G]}X)\subset D_{\mathrm{ctf}}(G,{}_AX).
\]

The duality formalism of (SGA 4 XVII, XVIII) extends without difficulty to $G$-$S$-schemes. Since the functor forgetting the action of $G$ is faithful, it is enough to extend naturally the definition of the “six operations” $(\lten,\RHom,f^*,f_*,f_!,f^!)$ and the canonical arrows considered in loc. cit. (base change, Künneth, duality, etc.). This extension presents no difficulty: for the definition of the functors $f_!$, $f^!$, it is enough to use equivariant compactifications. We leave to the reader the task of paraphrasing the definitions and results \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:subsection:6.2]{6.2} to \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:subsection:6.8]{6.8} in the setting of $G$-$S$-schemes.

If, for $i=1,2$, $X_i$ is a $G$-$S$-scheme, $c:C\to X=X_1\times_SX_2$ is a $G$-$S$-morphism, and $L_i\in\Ob D_{\mathrm{ctf}}(G,{}_AX_i)$, the elements of
\[
\Hom_A(c_1^*L_1,c_2^!L_2),
\]
where $\Hom_A$ is taken in the category $D(G,{}_A-)$, will be called equivariant cohomological correspondences from $L_1$ to $L_2$ with support in $c$. Note that, when $G$ acts trivially on $X_i$ $(i=1,2)$ and $L_i\in\Ob D_{\mathrm{ctf}}({}_{A[G]}X_i)$, the isomorphism \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:6.4.1]{6.4.1} in $D(G,X)$ refines to an isomorphism
\[
D_{A[G]}L_1\lten_{S,A[G]}L_2
\xrightarrow{\sim}
\RHom_{A[G]}(p_1^*L_1,p_2^!L_2).
\]
It follows that, in a $G$-equivariant situation of type \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:subsection:6.5]{6.5}, with trivial action of $G$ on the spaces, and with $L\in\Ob D_{\mathrm{ctf}}({}_{A[G]}X)\subset D_{\mathrm{ctf}}(G,{}_AX)$, one may, for
\[
u\in\Hom_{A[G]}(c_1^*L,c_2^!L),
\]
define a local term
\[
\Tr_{A[G]}(u)\in H^0(X^c,KA[G]_{X^c,L_{\natural}}),
\]
finer, in the evident sense, than the local term
\[
\Tr_A(u)\in H^0(G,X^c,KA_{X^c,L_{\natural}})
\]
obtained by using only finite tor-dimension of $L$ with respect to $A$.

\subsection*{6.10}\phantomsection\label{sga5:IIIB:subsection:6.10}
Let $X$, $c$ be as in \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:subsection:6.5]{6.5}, and let $L\in\Ob D_{\mathrm{ctf}}({}_{\Lambda[G]}X)$. For
\[
u\in\Hom_{\Lambda[G]}(c_1^*L,c_2^!L),
\]
\hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:6.5.9]{6.5.9} gives a local trace
\begin{equation}
\Tr_{\Lambda[G]}(u)\in
H^0(X^c,K\Lambda[G]_{X^c,\natural})
=H^0(X^c,K_{X^c}\otimes\Lambda[G_{\natural}]),
\tag{6.10.1}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:6.10.1}
\end{equation}
where $G_{\natural}$ is the set of conjugacy classes of $G$. For every $x\in G_{\natural}$, one may consider the “value of $\Tr_{\Lambda[G]}(u)$ at $x$,”
\[
\Tr_{\Lambda[G]}(u)(x)\in H^0(X^c,K_{X^c}),
\]
the image of $\Tr_{\Lambda[G]}(u)$ under the projection
\[
H^0(X^c,K_{X^c}\otimes\Lambda[G_{\natural}])
\longrightarrow H^0(X^c,K_{X^c})
\]
defined by $x$. It follows from \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:6.7.5]{6.7.5} and \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:5.11.2]{5.11.2} that
\begin{equation}
\Tr_{\Lambda}(u)=|G|\,\Tr_{\Lambda[G]}(u)(e),
\tag{6.10.2}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:6.10.2}
\end{equation}
where $e$ denotes the identity element of $G$.

More generally, let $g\in G$, let $Z_g$ be the centralizer of $g$, and let
\[
gu\in\Hom_{\Lambda[Z_g]}(c_1^*L,c_2^!L)
\]
be the correspondence obtained by composing $u$ with left multiplication by $g:c_1^*L\to c_1^*L$. We have $L\in\Ob D_{\mathrm{ctf}}({}_{\Lambda[Z_g]}X)$, and hence \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:6.10.2]{6.10.2} implies
\begin{equation}
\Tr_{\Lambda}(gu)=|Z_g|\,\Tr_{\Lambda[Z_g]}(gu)(e).
\tag{6.10.3}\label{sga5:IIIB:equation:6.10.3}
\end{equation}
By the same argument as in \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:proposition:5.11.3]{5.11.3}, one deduces from \hyperref[sga5:IIIB:equation:6.7.4]{6.7.4} the formula
\begin{equation}
\Tr_{\Lambda[Z_g]}(gu)(e)=\Tr_{\Lambda[G]}(u)(g^{-1}),
